72	## Week 2 — Connection manager — all datasource types (backend)
73	
74	**Exit gate:** All connection types configurable and testable via API; credentials stored in Key Vault.
75	
76	> **Auth-boundary discipline (per [ADR 0010](adr/0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md)):** new connection-CRUD endpoints (and every protected route from here on) depend on a generic internal "current user" dependency that returns DataQ's own `User` — they must NOT read MSAL token claims directly in route/service code. Cheap now; expensive to retrofit once dozens of endpoints exist. No new abstraction layer required, just the boundary.

1	# ADR 0004 — Unified `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction for ADF and Apache Airflow
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-05-24
5	- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	DataQ integrates with Azure Data Factory (ADF) and Apache Airflow. The original product framing listed ADF as a "5th datasource type," which conflates orchestration with data storage:
10	
11	- **Datasources** (Snowflake, ADLS Gen2, S3, Unity Catalog) are stores against which DQ checks are written.
12	- **Orchestrators** (ADF, Airflow) are workflow engines whose pipeline/DAG runs we observe and react to. We do not write DQ checks against the orchestrator itself.
13	
14	Treating orchestrators as datasources pollutes the suite/check editor, mixes unrelated concerns in the data model, and makes the upcoming Airflow integration look like a one-off rather than a sibling of ADF.
15	
16	## Decision
17	
18	**Treat ADF and Apache Airflow as orchestration integrations behind a single `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction. They are NOT datasources and never appear in the connection editor / check editor / suite model as a queryable target.**
19	
20	### Responsibilities (identical across both providers)
21	
22	1. **Monitor** pipeline/DAG runs → stored in `pipeline_runs` table with a `provider` column (`adf` | `airflow`) and `provider_run_id`.
23	2. **Detect failure** in near-real-time via provider-specific webhook channels (see table below).
24	3. **Trigger suite execution on successful completion** via `trigger_bindings`. The table has a surrogate primary key (`id`) and a non-unique composite **lookup index** on (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`) — one pipeline/DAG can map to many suites (one row per binding). Failure events alert the user but do NOT trigger suite runs (no point running checks on data that wasn't loaded).
25	
26	### Interface
27	
28	```
29	class OrchestrationProvider:
30	    def parse_event(self, payload: bytes, headers: dict) -> RunUpdate: ...
31	    def fetch_run_detail(self, provider_run_id: str) -> RunDetail: ...
32	    def list_recent_runs(self, since: datetime) -> list[RunUpdate]: ...
33	```
34	
35	Both `AdfProvider` and `AirflowProvider` implement this interface. Service code routes by `provider` enum, never by provider-specific branching.
36	
37	### Event channels per provider
38	
39	| Provider | Failure / success channel | Auth | Polling fallback |
40	|---|---|---|---|
41	| ADF | Azure Monitor alert rule → webhook `POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/adf` | Shared-secret header (Azure Monitor's only auth mode) | ADF REST API every 10 min |
42	| Airflow | DAG `on_success_callback` / `on_failure_callback` → webhook `POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/airflow` | HMAC-signed payload (signing key in Key Vault) | Airflow REST API `dagRuns` every 10 min |
43	
44	### UI surface
45	
46	Orchestrators appear ONLY in:
47	- The orchestration status panel on the results dashboard.
48	- The trigger-binding configuration UI.
49	
50	They do NOT appear in the connection editor's "pick a datasource" list or the check editor's table picker.
51	
52	## Consequences
53	
54	**Positive**
55	- Adding a third orchestration provider in the future (Prefect, Dagster, etc.) is a new `OrchestrationProvider` implementation — no data-model or UI churn.
56	- `pipeline_runs` stays clean as a single events stream regardless of provider.
57	- `trigger_bindings` is provider-agnostic from day one.
58	- Forces correct mental model — orchestrator is not a datasource.
59	
60	**Negative**
61	- Slightly more abstraction upfront in Week 2 vs hardcoding ADF, then refactoring when Airflow is added. Accepted because Airflow is in-scope for v1, not a hypothetical future.
62	- Airflow callback adoption is voluntary on the user side (we cannot mutate their DAGs). Mitigated by documenting the polling fallback and providing a copy-paste `dataq_airflow_callback` snippet.
63	
64	## Alternatives considered
65	
66	- **Treat ADF as a 5th datasource (original framing)** — rejected. Conflates orchestration with storage; pollutes the check editor and the data model.
67	- **Hardcode ADF in v1, abstract later when Airflow is added** — rejected. Refactoring after `pipeline_runs` has data is more expensive than designing the abstraction once upfront.
68	
69	## Related
70	
71	- `pipeline_runs` and `trigger_bindings` schema lands in Week 1/2.
72	- `OrchestrationProvider` interface lands in Week 2 alongside both implementations.
73	- Future: ADR 0006 (ADF webhook auth) and ADR 0007 (Airflow callback model) will detail the provider-specific event channels.
74	
```

{

  "old_string": "### UI surface\n\nOrchestrators appear ONLY in:",
  "new_string": "### Single-orchestrator-per-`(provider, env)` assumption\n\n`trigger_bindings` is keyed by (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`) → `suite_id`. This composite key **cannot disambiguate two instances of the same provider in the same environment** — e.g. two separate `airflow` deployments both serving `env=dev`. The agreed v1 shape therefore assumes **exactly one orchestrator instance per (`provider`, `env`) pair**.\n\n**Consequence:** to keep the binding lookup unambiguous, one of two things must hold:\n\n1. Connection-level CRUD enforces **uniqueness on (`type`, `env`) for orchestrator-typed connections** (an `adf`/`airflow` connection is singular per env), OR\n2. `trigger_bindings` grows a `connection_id` column so a binding names a specific orchestrator instance.\n\n**v1 decision:** take option 1 — document the assumption (this section) and **enforce `(type, env)` uniqueness at the connection-CRUD layer in Week 2** for orchestrator-typed connections. The schema bump (option 2) is **deferred to v1.1** and only revisited if multi-instance-per-env becomes a real requirement. Without this guard, a future contributor adding a second same-env Airflow connection would hit a silent binding collision rather than a validation error.\n\n### UI surface\n\nOrchestrators appear ONLY in:"
}

+### Single-orchestrator-per-`(provider, env)` assumption
+
+`trigger_bindings` is keyed by (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`) → `suite_id`. This composite key **cannot disambiguate two instances of the same provider in the same environment** — e.g. two separate `airflow` deployments both serving `env=dev`. +

The schema bump (option 2) is **deferred to v1.1** and only revisited if multi-instance-per-env becomes a real requirement. def _uuid_pk() -> Mapped[uuid.UUID]:
    return mapped_column(
        UUID(as_uuid=True),
        primary_key=True,
        server_default=text("gen_random_uuid()"),

def _created_at() -> Mapped[datetime]:
    return mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False, server_default=func.now())


def _updated_at() -> Mapped[datetime]:
    return mapped_column(
        DateTime(timezone=True),
        nullable=False,
        server_default=func.now(),
        onupdate=func.now(),
    )


def _in_check(column: str, values: tuple[str, ...], name: str) -> CheckConstraint:
    quoted = ", ".join(f"'{v}'" for v in values)
    return CheckConstraint(f"{column} IN ({quoted})", name=name)


class User(Base):

Now I've got a grounded picture. Key finding: **a lot of the foundation is already in place**, which shapes the plan:

- ✅ `Connection` model exists ([models.py:69](backend/app/db/models.py#L69)) — all 6 types in `CONNECTION_TYPES`, `env` CHECK, `secret_ref` nullable, `UniqueConstraint(name, env)`
- ✅ `get_current_user` dependency already returns DataQ's own `User` ([core/auth.py:172](backend/app/core/auth.py#L172)) — **the ADR 0010 auth boundary is already satisfied**; new routes just depend on it
- ✅ `SecretStore` abstraction (PR 3b) for credential write-through
- ✅ `datasources/{base,snowflake}.py` — `SnowflakeCheckRunner` we can reuse for the test endpoint
- ❌ No connection router / service / schemas yet · ❌ `orchestration/` package is greenfield

## Proposed Week 2 breakdown — 19 tasks → 7 PRs

**Track A — Connection manager (directly serves the Week 2 exit gate: "all connection types configurable + testable via API")**

| PR | Scope | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| **PR 5** | Connection CRUD **foundation** + Snowflake reference + `POST /connections/{id}/test`. Scaffolds Pydantic schemas, `connections` router, `connection_service`, credential write-through to `SecretStore`. Reuses existing `get_current_user`. **This is the template the other 3 datasources clone.** | — (unblocked) |
| **PR 6** | ADF connection CRUD + **`(type, env)` uniqueness guard** for orchestrator-typed rows (the #72 enforcement we just documented) | PR 5 |
| **PR 7** | ADLS Gen2 + S3 connection CRUD + GX `pandas_abs`/`pandas_s3` wiring + file-asset config model | PR 5 |
| **PR 8** | Unity Catalog / Databricks connection CRUD + UC auth test endpoint | PR 5 |
| **PR 9** | Cross-cutting: connection **re-auth** endpoint + resolve `secret_ref` nullability (now that all types exist, we know which need null — Airflow basic-poll / unauth S3) | PR 6–8 |

**Track B — Orchestration webhook receivers (additive; not in the exit gate)**

| PR | Scope | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| **PR 10** | `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction + ADF webhook receiver (`POST /events/adf`, shared-secret validation, parse, follow-up REST, upsert `pipeline_runs`) | **ADR 0006** |
| **PR 11** | Airflow webhook receiver (`POST /events/airflow` HMAC) + Airflow connection type + copy-paste callback snippet | **ADR 0007** |

## ⚠️ Two design decisions are overdue and gate Track B

[progress.md:378-379](docs/progress.md#L378-L379) flags both as due **"before Week 2 starts"** — and they need *your* call (you're the sole decider), I can't invent them:

- **ADR 0006** — ADF webhook auth: shared-secret header + Key Vault rotation policy
- **ADR 0007** — Airflow callback signing: HMAC scheme + signing-key handling + polling fallback

They **don't block PR 5** (Snowflake CRUD is clean), so we can start Track A immediately and slot the ADRs in before PR 10.

> AGENT

Good — let's lock these down before they hang over Track B. Both have their high-level shape already fixed in [ADR 0004](docs/adr/0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) and CLAUDE.md §4 (ADF = shared secret + 10-min REST fallback; Airflow = HMAC-signed + 10-min `dagRuns` fallback). What ADR 0006/0007 need to nail down are the **security specifics**. I'll bake in the conventional defaults (HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body, `X-DataQ-Signature` header, hex encoding, constant-time compare, signing material in Key Vault) — but three choices genuinely change the design and infra, so they're yours to call:

1	# Architecture Decision Records (ADRs)
2	
3	Each ADR captures a single significant architecture decision: the context, the decision, the consequences, and the alternatives considered. New ADRs are append-only — supersede an old decision by adding a new ADR and marking the old one's status as `Superseded by ADR-NNNN`.
4	
5	## Format
6	
7	- File name: `NNNN-short-kebab-slug.md` (zero-padded 4-digit sequence)
8	- Frontmatter fields: Status, Date, Deciders
9	- Sections: Context, Decision, Consequences, Alternatives considered, Related (optional)
10	- Keep each ADR short — 1–2 pages. If it grows past that, the decision is probably two decisions.
11	
12	## Index
13	
14	| # | Title | Status |
15	|---|---|---|
16	| [0001](0001-trunk-based-branching.md) | Trunk-based branching with squash-merge into `main` | Accepted |
17	| [0002](0002-conventional-commits.md) | Conventional commits for PR titles and commit messages | Accepted |
18	| [0003](0003-gx-only-for-v1.md) | GX-only for v1; DQX deferred to v1.1 for DLT/streaming | Accepted |
19	| [0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) | Unified `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction for ADF and Airflow | Accepted |
20	| [0009](0009-flat-monorepo-layout.md) | Repo layout — flat monorepo (`backend/` + `frontend/`) | Accepted |
21	| [0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) | Provider-agnostic infrastructure seams (Azure is the default, not the architecture) | Accepted |
22	| [0011](0011-extensibility-seams-for-deferred-integrations.md) | Extensibility seams for deferred connectors and integrations | Accepted |

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/docs/adr/0006-adf-webhook-authentication.md",
  "content": "# ADR 0006 — ADF webhook authentication (shared secret + hard-cutover rotation)\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2026-05-30\n- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771\n\n## Context\n\nPer [ADR 0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md), Azure Data Factory (ADF) pipeline run events reach DataQ via an Azure Monitor alert rule that POSTs to `POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/adf`. We must authenticate these inbound requests so a third party cannot forge pipeline events (which can trigger suite runs on successful-completion bindings).\n\nThe transport is constrained by Azure Monitor itself:\n\n- A **plain action-group webhook cannot attach arbitrary custom headers**. So a signed `X-DataQ-Signature`-style header (as used for Airflow in [ADR 0007](0007-airflow-callback-model.md)) is not directly available without an intermediary.\n- Azure Monitor's **Secure Webhook** action uses an Azure AD bearer token, which is stronger but requires an additional app registration + AAD wiring and shifts away from the shared-secret model framed in ADR 0004.\n\n## Decision\n\n**Authenticate the ADF webhook with a shared secret carried as a URL query parameter, validated constant-time against a single secret stored in Key Vault. Rotate by hard cutover. No request-freshness check in v1.**\n\n### Authentication\n\n- Azure Monitor posts to `https://<api-host>/api/v1/orchestration/events/adf?token=<secret>` over **HTTPS only**.\n- The endpoint reads `token`, compares it **constant-time** (`hmac.compare_digest`) against the secret resolved from Key Vault.\n- Missing / mismatched token → **401**. The token is never logged (PII/secret redaction at the logger level, per CLAUDE.md §10).\n- After successful auth, the endpoint returns **200 for all well-formed events** — including non-success and ignored events — so Azure Monitor does not enter a retry storm. Malformed body → 422.\n\n### Secret storage & config\n\n- Secret lives in Key Vault, surfaced to the app as `ADF_WEBHOOK_SECRET` via the existing `SecretStore` abstraction (PR 3b) — never inlined in config or committed.\n- The same value is configured on the Azure Monitor action group's webhook URL.\n\n### Rotation — hard cutover\n\n- **One active secret at a time.** Rotation = update the Key Vault secret **and** the Azure Monitor action-group webhook URL in lockstep.\n- A brief mismatch window during rotation may drop a webhook delivery; this is **acceptable because the 10-minute ADF REST polling fallback** (ADR 0004) recovers any missed `succeeded` runs. No dual-secret overlap logic in v1.\n\n### Replay / freshness — not enforced in v1\n\n- v1 validates the shared secret only; it does **not** reject stale `firedDateTime` timestamps.\n- Duplicate or replayed deliveries are neutralised downstream by the **idempotent upsert keyed on (`provider`, `provider_run_id`)** in `pipeline_runs` — a replayed event updates the same row and does not re-trigger a suite run.\n- Freshness enforcement is a documented hardening candidate, deferred until replay is shown to be a real risk.\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Positive**\n- Minimal infra — no extra Azure AD app registration, no Logic App hop. Ships inside the Week 2 webhook-receiver PR.\n- Constant-time compare + HTTPS-only + idempotent upsert covers the realistic threat model for a single-tenant v1.\n- Rotation is operationally simple; the polling fallback already exists as the safety net.\n\n**Negative**\n- The secret appears in the webhook **URL**, so it can surface in Azure Monitor action-group config and potentially some network/proxy logs. Mitigated by HTTPS, treating it as a rotatable credential, and never logging the query string on our side. A header- or AAD-based scheme would avoid this but at higher infra cost (see Alternatives).\n- Hard cutover can drop deliveries during the rotation window; accepted because polling backfills.\n- No replay window means a captured valid request could be replayed; impact is bounded to idempotent no-ops on existing `pipeline_runs` rows (no double-trigger).\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n- **Secure Webhook (Azure AD bearer token)** — rejected for v1. Strongest option and avoids secret-in-URL, but costs an extra app registration + AAD validation path and revises ADR 0004's shared-secret framing. Revisit if the security review before Week 7 deploy flags secret-in-URL as unacceptable.\n- **Logic App intermediary that injects a signed header** — rejected. Would let ADF and Airflow share an `X-DataQ-Signature` HMAC design (symmetric), but adds a Logic App resource + an operational hop to pay for and monitor.\n- **Dual-secret overlap rotation** — rejected for v1. Zero-downtime rotation, but the polling fallback already covers the small cutover gap, so the extra validation branch isn't worth it yet.\n\n## Related\n\n- [ADR 0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) — the unified `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction this webhook plugs into; `AdfProvider.parse_event` consumes the authenticated payload.\n- [ADR 0007](0007-airflow-callback-model.md) — the Airflow sibling; uses HMAC-signed-header auth because Airflow callbacks (unlike Azure Monitor) can set custom headers. Rotation (hard cutover) and replay (idempotent-upsert only, no freshness check) decisions are shared between the two.\n- Shared secret config (`ADF_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) and the receiver land in the Week 2 ADF webhook-receiver PR.\n- A security review before the Week 7 deploy should re-examine secret-in-URL exposure.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/docs/adr/0007-airflow-callback-model.md",
  "content": "# ADR 0007 — Airflow callback model (HMAC-signed webhook + polling fallback)\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2026-05-30\n- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771\n\n## Context\n\nPer [ADR 0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md), Apache Airflow DAG run events reach DataQ via DAG-level `on_success_callback` / `on_failure_callback` hooks that POST to `POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/airflow`. We must authenticate these requests so a third party cannot forge DAG events (successful-completion events can trigger suite runs via `trigger_bindings`).\n\nUnlike Azure Monitor (see [ADR 0006](0006-adf-webhook-authentication.md)), the Airflow callback is **code we author** as a copy-paste snippet for the user's DAGs, so it **can set custom headers and compute a signature**. This permits a stronger header-based HMAC scheme rather than a secret-in-URL.\n\nTwo constraints shape the design:\n\n- **We cannot mutate users' DAGs.** Adoption of the callback snippet is voluntary; some users will not add it. A polling fallback is therefore mandatory, not optional.\n- The signing key is a shared symmetric secret that must live in Key Vault on our side and in the user's Airflow environment.\n\n## Decision\n\n**Authenticate the Airflow webhook with an HMAC-SHA256 signature over the raw request body, carried in an `X-DataQ-Signature` header. Provide a copy-paste callback snippet; document the Airflow REST polling fallback for non-adopters. Rotate by hard cutover. No request-freshness check in v1.**\n\n### Authentication\n\n- The callback computes `HMAC-SHA256(signing_key, raw_body)`, hex-encoded, and sends it as **`X-DataQ-Signature: sha256=<hex>`** (GitHub-style prefix for forward compatibility with other digests).\n- The endpoint recomputes the HMAC over the **raw, unparsed body** and compares **constant-time** (`hmac.compare_digest`). Mismatch / missing signature → **401**.\n- Because the signature covers the raw body, every field — including a `timestamp` the snippet includes — is **authenticated** even though v1 does not act on freshness (leaves the door open to add a replay window later with no protocol change).\n- After successful auth, the endpoint returns **200 for all well-formed events**; malformed body → 422.\n\n### Callback snippet + polling fallback\n\n- Ship a copy-paste **`dataq_airflow_callback`** helper for users to wire into `on_success_callback` / `on_failure_callback`. It reads the signing key from the user's Airflow secret backend / env, never hardcoded.\n- For DAGs that don't adopt the snippet, the **Airflow REST API `dagRuns` polling fallback every 10 minutes** (ADR 0004) is the documented path to capture `success` runs. Webhook is the fast path; polling is the floor.\n- Per ADR 0004, **only success events trigger suite runs**; failure events alert the user but do not trigger.\n\n### Signing-key storage & config\n\n- Key lives in Key Vault, surfaced as `AIRFLOW_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_KEY` via the `SecretStore` abstraction (PR 3b). The same value is configured in the user's Airflow environment.\n\n### Rotation — hard cutover\n\n- **One active signing key at a time.** Rotation = update the Key Vault key **and** the user's Airflow-side key in lockstep.\n- A brief mismatch window may drop a callback delivery; **acceptable because the 10-minute `dagRuns` polling fallback** recovers missed `success` runs. No dual-key overlap logic in v1.\n\n### Replay / freshness — not enforced in v1\n\n- v1 validates the HMAC only; it does not reject stale `timestamp` values.\n- Duplicate / replayed deliveries are neutralised by the **idempotent upsert keyed on (`provider`, `provider_run_id`)** in `pipeline_runs` — no double-trigger.\n- Freshness enforcement is a documented hardening candidate (the timestamp is already signed), deferred until replay is shown to be a real risk.\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Positive**\n- Header-based HMAC over the raw body is a standard, well-understood scheme — no secret in the URL (improves on the ADF constraint).\n- Voluntary adoption is de-risked by the mandatory polling fallback; webhook is a latency optimisation, not a correctness dependency.\n- Symmetric with ADR 0006 on the operational decisions (hard-cutover rotation, idempotent-upsert dedup) — one mental model for both providers.\n\n**Negative**\n- Symmetric shared key means the user's Airflow environment holds signing material; a compromised Airflow could forge events. Bounded to that user's own DAGs in a single-tenant deployment; rotation is the mitigation.\n- Users who never adopt the snippet get **10-minute** worst-case detection latency instead of near-real-time. Documented, and acceptable for the DQ-trigger use case.\n- Hard cutover can drop deliveries during rotation; accepted because polling backfills.\n- No replay window; impact bounded to idempotent no-ops (no double-trigger).\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n- **Secret in URL query param (as ADF uses)** — rejected for Airflow. Since we author the callback, we can do proper header-based HMAC; no reason to accept the weaker secret-in-URL exposure here.\n- **Webhook-only, no polling fallback** — rejected. We can't guarantee users add the callback to every DAG; without polling, those runs would be invisible. ADR 0004 mandates the fallback.\n- **Dual-key overlap rotation** — rejected for v1, same rationale as ADR 0006: the polling fallback covers the cutover gap.\n- **Asymmetric signing (user holds a public key)** — rejected as over-engineered for single-tenant v1; symmetric HMAC + Key Vault is sufficient.\n\n## Related\n\n- [ADR 0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) — the `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction; `AirflowProvider.parse_event` consumes the authenticated payload, `list_recent_runs` drives the polling fallback.\n- [ADR 0006](0006-adf-webhook-authentication.md) — the ADF sibling; uses secret-in-URL because Azure Monitor webhooks can't set custom headers. Hard-cutover rotation and idempotent-upsert dedup (no freshness check) are shared decisions.\n- Signing-key config (`AIRFLOW_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_KEY`), the receiver, the `dataq_airflow_callback` snippet, and the Airflow connection type land in the Week 2 Airflow orchestration PR.\n"
}
```

Forced by the fact that plain Azure Monitor action-group webhooks can't attach custom headers.\n- **Rotation:** hard cutover, single active secret; cutover gap covered by the 10-min ADF REST polling fallback.\n- **Replay:** no v1 freshness check — idempotent upsert on `(provider, provider_run_id)` neutralizes duplicates.\n\n### [ADR 0007](docs/adr/0007-airflow-callback-model.md) — Airflow callback model\n- **Transport:** HMAC-SHA256 over the **raw body** in an `X-DataQ-Signature: sha256=<hex>` header (we author the callback, so a proper signed header is available).\n- **Adoption:** copy-paste `dataq_airflow_callback` snippet + **mandatory** 10-min `dagRuns` polling fallback (we can't mutate users' DAGs).\n- **Rotation / replay:** shares 0006's hard-cutover + idempotent-upsert decisions.\n\n## Decisions made by\n\n@TheurgicDuke771 (transport, rotation, replay все selected explicitly). Conventional defaults (HMAC-SHA256, header name, hex, constant-time compare, Key Vault storage) baked in.\n\n## Doc consistency\n\n- ADR index: 0006/0007 moved Pending → Accepted\n- CLAUDE.md §9 decision table updated\n- progress.md pending-decisions table marked resolved\n\n## Testing\n\nDocs-only. `pre-commit` clean (gitleaks passes — no real secret material in the ADRs).\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",

```
1	"""Week 1 exit-gate probe endpoint.
2	
3	POST seeds the probe fixtures, creates a queued Run, and dispatches the
4	``run_suite`` Celery task (GX → Snowflake DEV). GET reads a run back with its
5	results. This is a thin demonstrator of the full async path — not the general
6	run API, which arrives with suite/check CRUD in Weeks 3-5.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import uuid
12	from typing import Annotated, Any
13	
14	from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, status
15	from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict
16	from sqlalchemy import select
17	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
18	
19	from backend.app.core.auth import get_current_user
20	from backend.app.core.config import get_settings
21	from backend.app.db.models import Result, Run, User
22	from backend.app.db.session import get_db
23	from backend.app.services.probe import ensure_probe_fixtures
24	from backend.app.worker.tasks import run_suite
25	
26	router = APIRouter(tags=["probe"])
27	
28	
29	class ProbeRunResponse(BaseModel):
30	    run_id: uuid.UUID
31	    status: str
32	
33	
34	class CheckResultResponse(BaseModel):
35	    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)
36	
37	    check_id: uuid.UUID
38	    status: str
39	    observed_value: dict[str, Any] | None
40	    expected_value: dict[str, Any] | None
41	
42	
43	class RunStatusResponse(BaseModel):
44	    run_id: uuid.UUID
45	    status: str
46	    results: list[CheckResultResponse]
47	
48	
49	@router.post(
50	    "/_probe/snowflake-suite",
51	    response_model=ProbeRunResponse,
52	    status_code=status.HTTP_202_ACCEPTED,
53	    summary="Trigger the Snowflake probe suite",
54	    description=(
55	        "Seeds the dev Snowflake connection + canned suite, queues a run, and "
56	        "dispatches it to the Celery worker. Returns the run id to poll."
57	    ),
58	)
59	def trigger_snowflake_probe(
60	    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
61	    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
62	) -> ProbeRunResponse:
63	    settings = get_settings()
64	    _, suite, _ = ensure_probe_fixtures(db, user=current_user, settings=settings)
65	
66	    run = Run(suite_id=suite.id, status="queued", triggered_by=f"probe:{current_user.id}")
67	    db.add(run)
68	    db.commit()
69	    db.refresh(run)
70	
71	    table = settings.probe_snowflake_table or "UNSET"
72	    try:
73	        run_suite.delay(str(run.id), table)
74	    except Exception as exc:  # broker unreachable — don't leave the run stuck queued
75	        run.status = "failed"
76	        db.commit()
77	        raise HTTPException(
78	            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
79	            detail="failed to dispatch run",
80	        ) from exc
81	    return ProbeRunResponse(run_id=run.id, status=run.status)
82	
83	
84	@router.get(
85	    "/_probe/runs/{run_id}",
86	    response_model=RunStatusResponse,
87	    summary="Read a probe run and its results",
88	)
89	def get_probe_run(
90	    run_id: uuid.UUID,
91	    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
92	    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
93	) -> RunStatusResponse:
94	    run = db.get(Run, run_id)
95	    if run is None:
96	        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="run not found")
97	    results = list(db.scalars(select(Result).where(Result.run_id == run_id)))
98	    return RunStatusResponse(
99	        run_id=run.id,
100	        status=run.status,
101	        results=[CheckResultResponse.model_validate(r) for r in results],
102	    )
103	
```

1	"""Datasource adapter seam.
2	
3	Every datasource (Snowflake now; ADLS / S3 / Unity Catalog later) executes DQ
4	checks behind one ``CheckRunner`` interface that speaks GX-agnostic DTOs. The
5	GX-specific machinery lives entirely inside each adapter, so the run-service and
6	its tests depend only on the types here — never on Great Expectations internals.
7	This is also the seam that lets v1.1 swap GX for DQX on Unity Catalog (CLAUDE.md
8	§5) without rippling into the suite / check / result layer.
9	
10	`CheckSpec` goes in (a check pulled from the DB); `CheckOutcome` comes out, one
11	per check, shaped to map cleanly onto the `results` table columns. Adapters
12	translate GX results into these DTOs; tests provide a fake `CheckRunner` and
13	never touch a live datasource.
14	"""
15	
16	from __future__ import annotations
17	
18	from dataclasses import dataclass
19	from typing import Any, Protocol, runtime_checkable
20	
21	
22	@dataclass(frozen=True)
23	class CheckSpec:
24	    """One expectation to evaluate, sourced from a `checks` row.
25	
26	    `expectation_type` is the GX snake_case name (e.g.
27	    ``expect_column_values_to_not_be_null``); `kwargs` are its parameters
28	    (e.g. ``{"column": "id"}``). Adapters own the translation to the concrete
29	    GX expectation class.
30	    """
31	
32	    expectation_type: str
33	    kwargs: dict[str, Any]
34	
35	
36	@dataclass(frozen=True)
37	class CheckOutcome:
38	    """Result of one check, shaped for the `results` table.
39	
40	    `observed_value` / `expected_value` / `sample_failures` land in the
41	    matching JSONB columns. `sample_failures` may contain real data rows, so it
42	    is governed by the retention sweep and must only ever be logged through the
43	    PII-redacting structlog chain.
44	    """
45	
46	    expectation_type: str
47	    success: bool
48	    observed_value: dict[str, Any] | None = None
49	    expected_value: dict[str, Any] | None = None
50	    sample_failures: dict[str, Any] | None = None
51	
52	
53	@dataclass(frozen=True)
54	class SuiteOutcome:
55	    """Aggregate result of running a list of checks against one table."""
56	
57	    success: bool
58	    checks: list[CheckOutcome]
59	
60	
61	@runtime_checkable
62	class CheckRunner(Protocol):
63	    """Executes a set of checks against a single table and returns outcomes."""
64	
65	    def run_checks(
66	        self,
67	        *,
68	        table: str,
69	        schema: str | None,
70	        checks: list[CheckSpec],
71	    ) -> SuiteOutcome: ...
72	
```

1	"""Secret resolution abstraction.
2	
3	Two backends are supported, picked from `settings.secret_store`:
4	
5	- **EnvSecretStore** — reads secrets from env vars prefixed `KV_SECRET_`.
6	  Local dev only — convenient when running against `docker-compose` without
7	  an Azure tenant. Name normalisation: `snowflake-uat-finance` →
8	  env var `KV_SECRET_SNOWFLAKE_UAT_FINANCE`.
9	
10	- **AzureKeyVaultStore** — reads from Azure Key Vault via
11	  `azure-identity` (DefaultAzureCredential) + `azure-keyvault-secrets`.
12	  Production / staging. Real vault provisioning + tenant config land in
13	  Week 7 (deployment hardening); the code path is wired now so callers
14	  can take a dependency on `SecretStore` without waiting.
15	
16	The Azure SDK is **lazy-imported** so EnvSecretStore-only deployments
17	don't pay the import cost.
18	"""
19	
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	import os
23	import threading
24	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Final, Protocol, runtime_checkable
25	
26	from backend.app.core.config import Settings, get_settings
27	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
28	
29	if TYPE_CHECKING:
30	    from azure.keyvault.secrets import SecretClient
31	
32	log = get_logger(__name__)
33	
34	ENV_PREFIX: Final = "KV_SECRET_"
35	_AKV_MODE: Final = "azure_key_vault"
36	
37	
38	class SecretNotFoundError(Exception):
39	    """Raised when the requested secret is missing or unreadable."""
40	
41	
42	@runtime_checkable
43	class SecretStore(Protocol):
44	    def get(self, name: str) -> str: ...
45	
46	
47	def _env_key(name: str) -> str:
48	    return f"{ENV_PREFIX}{name.upper().replace('-', '_')}"
49	
50	
51	class EnvSecretStore:
52	    """Resolves secrets from `KV_SECRET_*` env vars. Local dev only."""
53	
54	    def get(self, name: str) -> str:
55	        key = _env_key(name)
56	        value = os.environ.get(key)
57	        if value is None:
58	            raise SecretNotFoundError(f"Env secret {key!r} not set (mapped from name={name!r})")
59	        return value
60	
61	
62	class AzureKeyVaultStore:
63	    """Resolves secrets from Azure Key Vault via DefaultAzureCredential."""
64	
65	    def __init__(self, vault_url: str) -> None:
66	        self._vault_url = vault_url
67	        self._client: SecretClient | None = None
68	        self._lock = threading.Lock()
69	
70	    def _client_lazy(self) -> SecretClient:
71	        if self._client is not None:
72	            return self._client
73	        with self._lock:
74	            if self._client is None:
75	                from azure.identity import DefaultAzureCredential
76	                from azure.keyvault.secrets import SecretClient
77	
78	                self._client = SecretClient(
79	                    vault_url=self._vault_url,
80	                    credential=DefaultAzureCredential(),
81	                )
82	            return self._client
83	
84	    def get(self, name: str) -> str:
85	        try:
86	            secret = self._client_lazy().get_secret(name)
87	        except Exception as exc:
88	            raise SecretNotFoundError(
89	                f"Key Vault secret {name!r} at {self._vault_url}: {exc}"
90	            ) from exc
91	        value = secret.value
92	        if value is None:
93	            raise SecretNotFoundError(f"Key Vault secret {name!r} has no value")
94	        return str(value)
95	
96	
97	_store_singleton: SecretStore | None = None
98	_store_lock = threading.Lock()
99	
100	
101	def _build_store(settings: Settings) -> SecretStore:
102	    if settings.secret_store == _AKV_MODE:
103	        if not settings.azure_key_vault_url:
104	            raise RuntimeError(f"secret_store={_AKV_MODE!r} requires AZURE_KEY_VAULT_URL")
105	        return AzureKeyVaultStore(settings.azure_key_vault_url)
106	    return EnvSecretStore()
107	
108	
109	def get_secret_store() -> SecretStore:
110	    """Return the configured store (cached after first call)."""
111	    global _store_singleton
112	    if _store_singleton is not None:
113	        return _store_singleton
114	    with _store_lock:
115	        if _store_singleton is None:
116	            settings = get_settings()
117	            _store_singleton = _build_store(settings)
118	            log.info("secret_store_initialized", backend=settings.secret_store)
119	        return _store_singleton
120	
121	
122	def reset_secret_store_cache() -> None:
123	    """Test-only: clear the cached store so the next call rebuilds it."""
124	    global _store_singleton
125	    with _store_lock:
126	        _store_singleton = None
127

1	"""Snowflake datasource adapter (GX Core 1.17).
2	
3	All Great-Expectations-specific machinery for Snowflake lives here, behind the
4	`CheckRunner` seam in ``base.py`` — per CLAUDE.md, the GX version-specific API
5	must not leak into the suite / check / result layer (GX v1 has drifted across
6	point releases).
7	
8	The full GX chain (``add_snowflake`` → ``add_table_asset`` →
9	``add_batch_definition_whole_table`` → ``ValidationDefinition.run``) connects to
10	Snowflake at asset-build time, so ``run_checks`` cannot run without a live
11	warehouse. Tests therefore exercise the GX-free parts directly — config
12	validation, connection-string building, the snake_case→GX-class translation,
13	and the GX-result→`CheckOutcome` mapping (fed a canned result) — and inject a
14	fake `CheckRunner` elsewhere. End-to-end validation against a real Snowflake DEV
15	warehouse is a tracked follow-up.
16	"""
17	
18	from __future__ import annotations
19	
20	from typing import Any
21	from urllib.parse import quote_plus
22	
23	import great_expectations as gx
24	import great_expectations.expectations as gxe
25	from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field
26	
27	from backend.app.core.secrets import SecretStore
28	from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome
29	
30	# GX result keys that describe failing rows — copied into CheckOutcome.sample_failures.
31	# These may contain real data, so they only ever reach logs via the redactor.
32	_SAMPLE_KEYS = ("partial_unexpected_list", "unexpected_count", "unexpected_percent")
33	
34	# GX injects internal bookkeeping keys into expectation_config.kwargs at run
35	# time (e.g. batch_id); strip them so expected_value persists only the check's
36	# own parameters.
37	_GX_INTERNAL_KWARGS = frozenset({"batch_id"})
38	
39	
40	class UnknownExpectationError(ValueError):
41	    """Raised when a check's expectation_type has no matching GX expectation."""
42	
43	
44	class SnowflakeConfig(BaseModel):
45	    """Non-secret Snowflake connection config (the password comes from secrets).
46	
47	    Maps from ``Connection.config``. ``schema`` is aliased to ``schema_`` to
48	    avoid shadowing pydantic's ``BaseModel.schema``.
49	    """
50	
51	    model_config = ConfigDict(populate_by_name=True, extra="forbid")
52	
53	    account: str
54	    user: str
55	    database: str
56	    schema_: str = Field(alias="schema")
57	    warehouse: str
58	    role: str | None = None
59	
60	
61	def build_connection_string(config: SnowflakeConfig, password: str) -> str:
62	    """Assemble a snowflake-sqlalchemy URL. User/password/params are URL-encoded."""
63	    params = {"warehouse": config.warehouse}
64	    if config.role:
65	        params["role"] = config.role
66	    query = "&".join(f"{key}={quote_plus(value)}" for key, value in params.items())
67	    return (
68	        f"snowflake://{quote_plus(config.user)}:{quote_plus(password)}"
69	        f"@{config.account}/{config.database}/{config.schema_}?{query}"
70	    )
71	
72	
73	def _expectation_class_name(expectation_type: str) -> str:
74	    """snake_case GX type → PascalCase class name.
75	
76	    ``expect_column_values_to_not_be_null`` → ``ExpectColumnValuesToNotBeNull``.
77	    """
78	    return "".join(part.title() for part in expectation_type.split("_"))
79	
80	
81	def _to_gx_expectation(spec: CheckSpec) -> Any:
82	    class_name = _expectation_class_name(spec.expectation_type)
83	    expectation_cls = getattr(gxe, class_name, None)
84	    if expectation_cls is None:
85	        raise UnknownExpectationError(
86	            f"Unknown expectation_type {spec.expectation_type!r} (no gx class {class_name!r})"
87	        )
88	    return expectation_cls(**spec.kwargs)
89	
90	
91	def _extract_sample_failures(result: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
92	    sample = {key: result[key] for key in _SAMPLE_KEYS if key in result}
93	    return sample or None
94	
95	
96	def _expected_value(kwargs: Any) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
97	    cleaned = {key: value for key, value in dict(kwargs).items() if key not in _GX_INTERNAL_KWARGS}
98	    return cleaned or None
99	
100	
101	def to_suite_outcome(gx_result: Any) -> SuiteOutcome:
102	    """Map a GX ExpectationSuiteValidationResult onto our GX-agnostic DTO.
103	
104	    Kept module-level (not a private method) so it is unit-testable with a
105	    constructed GX result, no Snowflake connection required.
106	    """
107	    outcomes: list[CheckOutcome] = []
108	    for check_result in gx_result.results:
109	        config = check_result.expectation_config
110	        detail: dict[str, Any] = check_result.result or {}
111	        observed = (
112	            {"observed_value": detail["observed_value"]} if "observed_value" in detail else None
113	        )
114	        outcomes.append(
115	            CheckOutcome(
116	                expectation_type=config.type,
117	                success=bool(check_result.success),
118	                observed_value=observed,
119	                expected_value=_expected_value(config.kwargs) if config.kwargs else None,
120	                sample_failures=_extract_sample_failures(detail),
121	            )
122	        )
123	    return SuiteOutcome(success=bool(gx_result.success), checks=outcomes)
124	
125	
126	class SnowflakeCheckRunner:
127	    """`CheckRunner` for Snowflake. Building the asset connects to the warehouse."""
128	
129	    def __init__(self, config: SnowflakeConfig, password: str) -> None:
130	        self._config = config
131	        self._connection_string = build_connection_string(config, password)
132	
133	    def run_checks(
134	        self,
135	        *,
136	        table: str,
137	        schema: str | None,
138	        checks: list[CheckSpec],
139	    ) -> SuiteOutcome:
140	        context = gx.get_context(mode="ephemeral")
141	        datasource = context.data_sources.add_snowflake(
142	            name=f"sf-{table}",
143	            connection_string=self._connection_string,
144	        )
145	        asset = datasource.add_table_asset(
146	            name=table,
147	            table_name=table,
148	            schema_name=schema or self._config.schema_,
149	        )
150	        batch_definition = asset.add_batch_definition_whole_table(name="whole_table")
151	        # GX 1.17 requires the suite and validation definition to be registered
152	        # on the context before run(); a free-standing ValidationDefinition.run()
153	        # raises ValidationDefinitionRelatedResourcesFreshnessError. The context
154	        # is ephemeral (per-run), so the fixed names never collide across runs.
155	        suite = context.suites.add(
156	            gx.ExpectationSuite(
157	                name=f"suite-{table}",
158	                expectations=[_to_gx_expectation(check) for check in checks],
159	            )
160	        )
161	        validation_definition = context.validation_definitions.add(
162	            gx.ValidationDefinition(
163	                name=f"vd-{table}",
164	                data=batch_definition,
165	                suite=suite,
166	            )
167	        )
168	        result = validation_definition.run(result_format="COMPLETE")
169	        return to_suite_outcome(result)
170	
171	
172	def build_snowflake_runner(
173	    *,
174	    config: dict[str, Any],
175	    secret_ref: str | None,
176	    secret_store: SecretStore,
177	) -> SnowflakeCheckRunner:
178	    """Build a runner from a `Connection` row's config + secret_ref.
179	
180	    Takes primitives (not the ORM model) to keep the adapter decoupled from the
181	    DB layer.
182	    """
183	    if not secret_ref:
184	        raise ValueError("Snowflake connection requires secret_ref for the password")
185	    sf_config = SnowflakeConfig.model_validate(config)
186	    password = secret_store.get(secret_ref)
187	    return SnowflakeCheckRunner(sf_config, password)
188	
```

1	from typing import Any
2	
3	from fastapi import FastAPI, Request, status
4	from fastapi.exceptions import RequestValidationError
5	from fastapi.responses import JSONResponse
6	from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
7	from starlette.exceptions import HTTPException as StarletteHTTPException
8	
9	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
10	
11	logger = get_logger(__name__)
12	
13	
14	class ErrorBody(BaseModel):
15	    code: str
16	    message: str
17	    detail: dict[str, Any] = Field(default_factory=dict)
18	
19	
20	class ErrorResponse(BaseModel):
21	    error: ErrorBody
22	
23	
24	class DataQError(Exception):
25	    status_code: int = status.HTTP_500_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR
26	    code: str = "internal_error"
27	
28	    def __init__(
29	        self,
30	        message: str,
31	        *,
32	        code: str | None = None,
33	        status_code: int | None = None,
34	        detail: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
35	    ) -> None:
36	        super().__init__(message)
37	        self.message = message
38	        if code is not None:
39	            self.code = code
40	        if status_code is not None:
41	            self.status_code = status_code
42	        self.detail: dict[str, Any] = detail or {}
43	
44	
45	def _envelope(code: str, message: str, detail: dict[str, Any] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
46	    return ErrorResponse(
47	        error=ErrorBody(code=code, message=message, detail=detail or {})
48	    ).model_dump()
49	
50	
51	async def _dataq_error_handler(_request: Request, exc: Exception) -> JSONResponse:
52	    assert isinstance(exc, DataQError)
53	    logger.warning("dataq_error", code=exc.code, message=exc.message, status=exc.status_code)
54	    return JSONResponse(
55	        status_code=exc.status_code,
56	        content=_envelope(exc.code, exc.message, exc.detail),
57	    )
58	
59	
60	async def _http_exception_handler(_request: Request, exc: Exception) -> JSONResponse:
61	    assert isinstance(exc, StarletteHTTPException)
62	    return JSONResponse(
63	        status_code=exc.status_code,
64	        content=_envelope("http_error", str(exc.detail)),
65	    )
66	
67	
68	async def _validation_exception_handler(_request: Request, exc: Exception) -> JSONResponse:
69	    assert isinstance(exc, RequestValidationError)
70	    return JSONResponse(
71	        status_code=status.HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY,
72	        content=_envelope(
73	            "validation_error", "Request validation failed", {"errors": exc.errors()}
74	        ),
75	    )
76	
77	
78	async def _unhandled_exception_handler(_request: Request, exc: Exception) -> JSONResponse:
79	    logger.exception("unhandled_exception", error_type=type(exc).__name__)
80	    return JSONResponse(
81	        status_code=status.HTTP_500_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR,
82	        content=_envelope("internal_error", "Internal server error"),
83	    )
84	
85	
86	def register_exception_handlers(app: FastAPI) -> None:
87	    app.add_exception_handler(DataQError, _dataq_error_handler)
88	    app.add_exception_handler(StarletteHTTPException, _http_exception_handler)
89	    app.add_exception_handler(RequestValidationError, _validation_exception_handler)
90	    app.add_exception_handler(Exception, _unhandled_exception_handler)
91

> AGENT

I now have everything I need. Two findings shape the plan:

1. **`SecretStore` is read-only** (`get` only). Connection CRUD has to get credentials *into* the store — that's the one real fork in this PR.
2. **No schema change needed** — the `Connection` model already has every column (`config` JSONB, `secret_ref`, `created_by`, `UniqueConstraint(name, env)`). **PR 5 needs no Alembic migration.**

Here's the file-level plan.

---

# PR 5 — Connection CRUD foundation + Snowflake + test endpoint

**Goal:** the generic connection-CRUD machinery, with Snowflake as the first concrete type and a `POST /test` connectivity check. This is the template PRs 6–8 (ADF, ADLS/S3, UC) clone.

**Branch:** `feature/connection-crud-snowflake` · **Migration:** none (model already complete) · **Auth:** reuses existing `get_current_user` (ADR 0010 boundary already satisfied)

## New files

**`backend/app/services/connection_service.py`** — business logic, FastAPI-free (unit-testable like `run_service`)
- `create_connection`, `list_connections`, `get_connection`, `update_connection`, `delete_connection`, `test_connection`
- A **per-type config-validation registry** `{"snowflake": SnowflakeConfig}` — reuses the existing `SnowflakeConfig` from [datasources/snowflake.py:44](backend/app/datasources/snowflake.py#L44). PRs 6–8 just add one entry each. Unknown/unsupported type → `DataQError`.
- **Secret write-through** on create/update (see fork below); generates a deterministic `secret_ref` (e.g. `conn-{type}-{env}-{name}`).
- `test_connection` builds the Snowflake engine, runs `SELECT 1` with a connect timeout, returns ok / structured failure.
- Raises typed `DataQError` subclasses: `ConnectionNotFoundError` (404), `ConnectionConfigInvalidError` (422), `ConnectionTestFailedError` (502).

**`backend/app/api/v1/connections.py`** — router (`tags=["connections"]`), Pydantic schemas inline (matches [probe.py](backend/app/api/v1/probe.py) convention)
- Schemas: `ConnectionCreate` (name, type, env, config, secret payload), `ConnectionUpdate` (partial), `ConnectionRead` (**never** emits secret material — exposes `has_secret: bool`, not `secret_ref` value), `ConnectionTestResult`
- Routes (all `Depends(get_current_user)`, `get_db`, `get_secret_store`):

| Method | Path | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `POST` | `/connections` | 201 `ConnectionRead` |
| `GET` | `/connections` | 200 `list[ConnectionRead]` (optional `?type=&env=` filters) |
| `GET` | `/connections/{id}` | 200 / 404 |
| `PATCH` | `/connections/{id}` | 200 / 404 |
| `DELETE` | `/connections/{id}` | 204 / 404 |
| `POST` | `/connections/{id}/test` | 200 `ConnectionTestResult` |

- The `/test` route is a **sync `def`** (FastAPI runs it in a threadpool → Snowflake's blocking connect won't stall the event loop).

## Edited files

**`backend/app/datasources/snowflake.py`** — add module-level `test_connection(config, password) -> None` (raises on failure): a lightweight `create_engine(...).connect()` + `SELECT 1` with a connect timeout. Keeps GX out of the path; reuses `build_connection_string`.

**`backend/app/main.py`** — `app.include_router(connections_router.router, prefix="/api/v1")` (next to the `me`/`probe` includes at [main.py:81](backend/app/main.py#L81)).

**`backend/app/core/secrets.py`** — *only if we take Option A below* — extend the `SecretStore` Protocol + both stores with `set(name, value)`.

## New tests

- **`backend/tests/services/test_connection_service.py`** — CRUD happy paths, config validation errors, not-found, secret write-through (fake store), `test_connection` ok/fail via a fake (no live Snowflake), following the `test_run_service.py` fake-injection pattern.
- **`backend/tests/api/test_connections.py`** — endpoint CRUD against the real-Postgres fixture (mirrors [test_probe.py](backend/tests/api/test_probe.py)): 201/200/404/422/204, test ok/fail, secret never leaks in responses.

## Manual verification (working agreement)
`docker-compose up` → `curl` create a Snowflake DEV connection → `POST /test` → list → delete. Document the session in the PR.

Good — **Option A locked**: I'll extend `SecretStore` with `set(name, value)` (`AzureKeyVaultStore` → `set_secret`, `EnvSecretStore` → dev-only `os.environ` write), and create accepts the password in-body and write-throughs to a generated `secret_ref`.

Now, your question — **and it's the right one to ask, because my draft plan quietly dodged it.**

## Honest answer: no, my draft did *not* create a wrapper class

What I wrote above uses the lightweight version — a **dict registry** `{"snowflake": SnowflakeConfig}` for config validation, plus a standalone `test_connection` function. That's the minimal thing that works for a Snowflake-only PR.

But that's arguably the wrong call here, and here's why I'd reconsider:

The type-varying behavior across connections isn't just config shape — it's **three things that diverge per type**:
| | Snowflake | ADF | ADLS/S3 | Unity Catalog |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **config schema** | account/user/db/… | subscription + SP | account URL + container | workspace + warehouse |
| **how you test it** | `SELECT 1` | ARM token call | list container | PAT + warehouse ping |
| **secret shape** | password | SP secret | SAS / managed-id | PAT token |

A dict-of-config-models only abstracts the *first* row. The test logic and secret handling would end up as `if type == ...` branches in the service — which is exactly the provider-branching anti-pattern CLAUDE.md §11 warns against, just for datasources instead of orchestrators.

And critically: **all four types land within Week 2-3.** This isn't a speculative second implementation (the ADR 0011 "defer it" case) — ADF/ADLS/S3/UC are imminent. That's the textbook condition for building the seam *now*.

## So I'd recommend a thin per-type adapter Protocol

Consistent with the seams the repo already has (`CheckRunner`, `OrchestrationProvider`, `SecretStore`):

```python
# datasources/base.py — alongside CheckRunner
class ConnectionAdapter(Protocol):
    def validate_config(self, raw: dict) -> BaseModel: ...   # parse + validate
    def test(self, config: BaseModel, secret: str) -> None: ...  # raise on failure
```

Snowflake is the reference impl in PR 5; PRs 6-8 each register one adapter and **touch zero CRUD code**. It's only marginally more than the registry, and it avoids a guaranteed retrofit in PR 6.

This changes the plan's structure, so let me confirm the direction before I finalize:

# PR 5 — Connection CRUD foundation + Snowflake + test endpoint (final)

**Branch:** `feature/connection-crud-snowflake` · **Migration:** none · **Auth:** existing `get_current_user`

## The seam (new abstraction, Snowflake = reference impl)

**`backend/app/datasources/base.py`** *(edit)* — add alongside `CheckRunner`:
```python
class ConnectionAdapter(Protocol):
    def validate_config(self, raw: dict) -> BaseModel: ...   # parse/validate → typed config
    def test(self, config: BaseModel, secret: str) -> None: ...  # raise on connectivity failure
```

**`backend/app/datasources/snowflake.py`** *(edit)* — add `SnowflakeConnectionAdapter`:
- `validate_config` → reuses existing `SnowflakeConfig.model_validate`
- `test` → `create_engine(build_connection_string(...)).connect()` + `SELECT 1` with a connect timeout (no GX in the path; reuses `build_connection_string`)

**`backend/app/datasources/registry.py`** *(new)* — `get_connection_adapter(type) -> ConnectionAdapter`, backed by `{"snowflake": SnowflakeConnectionAdapter()}`. **This dict is the only thing PRs 6-8 touch to add a type.**

## Service + API

**`backend/app/services/connection_service.py`** *(new)* — FastAPI-free, unit-testable:
- `create / list / get / update / delete / test`
- dispatches to `get_connection_adapter(type)` for validate + test — **zero `if type ==` branching**
- secret **write-through** on create/update via the new `SecretStore.set`, storing a generated `secret_ref` (`conn-{type}-{env}-{name}`)
- typed `DataQError` subclasses: `ConnectionNotFoundError` (404), `ConnectionConfigInvalidError` (422), `ConnectionTestFailedError` (502)

**`backend/app/api/v1/connections.py`** *(new)* — router `tags=["connections"]`, inline Pydantic schemas; `ConnectionRead` emits `has_secret: bool`, **never** secret material. Routes: `POST` / `GET` (+`?type=&env=`) / `GET {id}` / `PATCH {id}` / `DELETE {id}` / `POST {id}/test` (sync `def` → threadpool).

## Plumbing + secrets

**`backend/app/core/secrets.py`** *(edit)* — `SecretStore.set(name, value)` on the Protocol; `AzureKeyVaultStore.set` → `client.set_secret`; `EnvSecretStore.set` → `os.environ` (dev-only, documented non-persistent).

**`backend/app/main.py`** *(edit)* — include the connections router.

## Tests
- `tests/services/test_connection_service.py` — CRUD, validation/not-found errors, secret write-through (fake store), test ok/fail (fake adapter)
- `tests/api/test_connections.py` — endpoint CRUD against real-Postgres fixture; secret-never-leaks assertion
- `tests/core/test_secrets.py` *(edit)* — `set`/`get` roundtrip
- `tests/datasources/test_snowflake.py` *(edit)* — `SnowflakeConnectionAdapter.validate_config` (GX-free)

## Commit slicing (one-functionality-per-commit)
1. `SecretStore.set` write capability (+ tests)
2. `ConnectionAdapter` seam + Snowflake adapter + registry (+ tests)
3. `connection_service` CRUD + test (+ tests)
4. `connections` router + main wiring (+ API tests)

## Manual verification
`docker-compose up` → curl create Snowflake DEV connection → `POST /test` → list → delete. Document in PR.

42	# pure-unit suite anywhere. CI provides an ephemeral Postgres service.
43	
44	TEST_DATABASE_URL = os.environ.get("TEST_DATABASE_URL")

  "old_string": "@runtime_checkable\nclass CheckRunner(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Executes a set of checks against a single table and returns outcomes.\"\"\"\n\n    def run_checks(\n        self,\n        *,\n        table: str,\n        schema: str | None,\n        checks: list[CheckSpec],\n    ) -> SuiteOutcome: ...",

Each adapter owns\n    its own pydantic config model; both methods take the raw config dict so the\n    adapter is the single source of truth for that type's shape.\n\n    `validate_config` parses + validates a stored/incoming config (raising\n    pydantic ``ValidationError`` on bad input) and returns the normalised model.\n    `test` resolves connectivity against the live datasource using the config +\n    its secret, raising on failure. Adapters never touch the DB or SecretStore —\n    the caller resolves the secret and hands it in.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def validate_config(self, raw: dict[str, Any]) -> BaseModel: ...\n\n    def test(self, raw: dict[str, Any], secret: str) -> None: ..."
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/datasources/snowflake.py",
  "old_string": "def build_snowflake_runner(\n    *,\n    config: dict[str, Any],\n    secret_ref: str | None,\n    secret_store: SecretStore,\n) -> SnowflakeCheckRunner:\n    \"\"\"Build a runner from a `Connection` row's config + secret_ref.\n\n    Takes primitives (not the ORM model) to keep the adapter decoupled from the\n    DB layer.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not secret_ref:\n        raise ValueError(\"Snowflake connection requires secret_ref for the password\")\n    sf_config = SnowflakeConfig.model_validate(config)\n    password = secret_store.get(secret_ref)\n    return SnowflakeCheckRunner(sf_config, password)",
  "new_string": "def build_snowflake_runner(\n    *,\n    config: dict[str, Any],\n    secret_ref: str | None,\n    secret_store: SecretStore,\n) -> SnowflakeCheckRunner:\n    \"\"\"Build a runner from a `Connection` row's config + secret_ref.\n\n    Takes primitives (not the ORM model) to keep the adapter decoupled from the\n    DB layer.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not secret_ref:\n        raise ValueError(\"Snowflake connection requires secret_ref for the password\")\n    sf_config = SnowflakeConfig.model_validate(config)\n    password = secret_store.get(secret_ref)\n    return SnowflakeCheckRunner(sf_config, password)\n\n\n# Snowflake connector timeouts (seconds) for the connectivity test — fail fast\n# rather than hanging the request thread on an unreachable account.\n_TEST_LOGIN_TIMEOUT = 10\n_TEST_NETWORK_TIMEOUT = 10\n\n\nclass SnowflakeConnectionAdapter:\n    \"\"\"`ConnectionAdapter` for Snowflake — config validation + a SELECT 1 test.\"\"\"\n\n    def validate_config(self, raw: dict[str, Any]) -> SnowflakeConfig:\n        return SnowflakeConfig.model_validate(raw)\n\n    def test(self, raw: dict[str, Any], secret: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Open a connection and run ``SELECT 1``; raise on any failure.\n\n        Deliberately GX-free — a lightweight connectivity probe, not a suite run.\n        \"\"\"\n        from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text\n\n        config = self.validate_config(raw)\n        engine = create_engine(\n            build_connection_string(config, secret),\n            connect_args={\n                \"login_timeout\": _TEST_LOGIN_TIMEOUT,\n                \"network_timeout\": _TEST_NETWORK_TIMEOUT,\n            },\n        )\n        try:\n            with engine.connect() as conn:\n                conn.execute(text(\"SELECT 1\"))\n        finally:\n            engine.dispose()"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/datasources/registry.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Connection-type → adapter registry.\n\nThe single place that maps a ``Connection.type`` to its `ConnectionAdapter`.\nAdding a datasource type (ADF, ADLS Gen2, S3, Unity Catalog — Weeks 2-3) is a\none-line entry here plus the adapter itself; connection-CRUD service code stays\nuntouched, dispatching through `get_connection_adapter`.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom backend.app.datasources.base import ConnectionAdapter\nfrom backend.app.datasources.snowflake import SnowflakeConnectionAdapter\n\n\nclass UnsupportedConnectionTypeError(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when no adapter is registered for a connection type.\"\"\"\n\n\n_ADAPTERS: dict[str, ConnectionAdapter] = {\n    \"snowflake\": SnowflakeConnectionAdapter(),\n}\n\n\ndef get_connection_adapter(conn_type: str) -> ConnectionAdapter:\n    adapter = _ADAPTERS.get(conn_type)\n    if adapter is None:\n        raise UnsupportedConnectionTypeError(\n            f\"No connection adapter registered for type {conn_type!r}\"\n        )\n    return adapter\n"
}

1	"""Execute a suite's checks via a datasource adapter and persist the results.
2	
3	This is the GX-agnostic core of a run: it drives the `Run` lifecycle, calls a
4	`CheckRunner` (injected — Snowflake in production, a fake in tests), and maps the
5	returned `SuiteOutcome` onto `Result` rows. GX/Snowflake specifics live behind
6	the adapter; this layer only knows the DTOs in ``datasources.base``.
7	
8	Run.status describes *execution*, not data quality: a run that completes is
9	``succeeded`` even when checks fail (the failures live in ``Result.status`` /
10	``SuiteOutcome.success``). ``failed`` means the run could not execute — the
11	adapter raised (e.g. could not reach the warehouse).
12	"""
13	
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	from datetime import UTC, datetime
17	
18	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
19	
20	from backend.app.core.jsonsafe import sanitize_json
21	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
22	from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckRunner, CheckSpec
23	from backend.app.db.models import Check, Result, Run
24	
25	log = get_logger(__name__)
26	
27	
28	def _now() -> datetime:
29	    return datetime.now(UTC)
30	
31	
32	def execute_run(
33	    session: Session,
34	    *,
35	    run: Run,
36	    checks: list[Check],
37	    runner: CheckRunner,
38	    table: str,
39	    schema: str | None = None,
40	) -> Run:
41	    """Run ``checks`` against ``table`` via ``runner`` and persist the outcome.
42	
43	    ``run`` must already be persisted (it carries the id the results link to).
44	    Returns the same `Run`, updated to ``succeeded`` or ``failed``.
45	    """
46	    run.status = "running"
47	    run.started_at = _now()
48	    session.commit()
49	    log.info(
50	        "run_started",
51	        run_id=str(run.id),
52	        suite_id=str(run.suite_id),
53	        n_checks=len(checks),
54	        table=table,
55	    )
56	
57	    specs = [CheckSpec(expectation_type=c.expectation_type, kwargs=dict(c.config)) for c in checks]
58	
59	    # Everything from here — running the adapter, building rows, and persisting
60	    # them — is guarded so any failure drives the run to a terminal 'failed'
61	    # state. Without this, a DB error during add_all/commit would leave the run
62	    # stuck in 'running' forever. rollback() discards any partial result inserts
63	    # before we record the failure.
64	    try:
65	        outcome = runner.run_checks(table=table, schema=schema, checks=specs)
66	        rows = [
67	            Result(
68	                run_id=run.id,
69	                check_id=check.id,
70	                status="passed" if check_outcome.success else "failed",
71	                observed_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.observed_value),
72	                expected_value=sanitize_json(check_outcome.expected_value),
73	                sample_failures=sanitize_json(check_outcome.sample_failures),
74	            )
75	            for check, check_outcome in zip(checks, outcome.checks, strict=True)
76	        ]
77	        session.add_all(rows)
78	        run.status = "succeeded"
79	        run.finished_at = _now()
80	        session.commit()
81	    except Exception:
82	        session.rollback()
83	        run.status = "failed"
84	        run.finished_at = _now()
85	        session.commit()
86	        log.exception("run_failed", run_id=str(run.id), table=table)
87	        return run
88	
89	    log.info(
90	        "run_completed",
91	        run_id=str(run.id),
92	        suite_success=outcome.success,
93	        n_results=len(rows),
94	    )
95	    return run
96	
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/connection_service.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Connection CRUD + connectivity test, datasource-type-agnostic.\n\nDrives the `connections` table and dispatches type-specific behaviour through\nthe `ConnectionAdapter` registry — so this layer never branches on\n``connection.type``. Credentials are written through the `SecretStore`\n(`set`) and only ever referenced by `Connection.secret_ref`; the plaintext\nsecret is never stored on the row or logged.\n\nFastAPI-free by design (like `run_service`): takes a `Session` + `SecretStore`,\nreturns ORM models, raises `DataQError` subclasses. The API layer owns\nrequest/response shapes and dependency wiring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport uuid\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom pydantic import ValidationError\nfrom sqlalchemy import select\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom backend.app.core.errors import DataQError\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import get_logger\nfrom backend.app.core.secrets import SecretNotFoundError, SecretStore\nfrom backend.app.datasources.registry import (\n    UnsupportedConnectionTypeError,\n    get_connection_adapter,\n)\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Connection\n\nlog = get_logger(__name__)\n\n\nclass ConnectionNotFoundError(DataQError):\n    status_code = 404\n    code = \"connection_not_found\"\n\n\nclass ConnectionConfigInvalidError(DataQError):\n    status_code = 422\n    code = \"connection_config_invalid\"\n\n\nclass ConnectionTestFailedError(DataQError):\n    status_code = 502\n    code = \"connection_test_failed\"\n\n\ndef _validated_config(conn_type: str, config: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Reject an unknown type or a config that fails its adapter's schema.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        adapter = get_connection_adapter(conn_type)\n    except UnsupportedConnectionTypeError as exc:\n        raise ConnectionConfigInvalidError(str(exc), detail={\"type\": conn_type}) from exc\n    try:\n        adapter.validate_config(config)\n    except ValidationError as exc:\n        raise ConnectionConfigInvalidError(\n            f\"Invalid config for {conn_type!r} connection\",\n            detail={\"errors\": exc.errors()},\n        ) from exc\n\n\ndef create_connection(\n    session: Session,\n    *,\n    name: str,\n    conn_type: str,\n    env: str,\n    config: dict[str, Any],\n    secret: str | None,\n    created_by: uuid.UUID,\n    secret_store: SecretStore,\n) -> Connection:\n    \"\"\"Validate, persist, and (if a secret is given) write its credential.\n\n    The secret_ref is derived from the row's own id (``conn-<uuid>``) — unique\n    and safe as a Key Vault secret name. The credential is written through the\n    store; only the ref is persisted on the row.\n    \"\"\"\n    _validated_config(conn_type, config)\n\n    conn = Connection(\n        name=name,\n        type=conn_type,\n        env=env,\n        config=config,\n        secret_ref=None,\n        created_by=created_by,\n    )\n    session.add(conn)\n    session.flush()  # assign conn.id for the secret_ref\n\n    if secret is not None:\n        secret_ref = f\"conn-{conn.id}\"\n        secret_store.set(secret_ref, secret)\n        conn.secret_ref = secret_ref\n\n    session.commit()\n    session.refresh(conn)\n    log.info(\"connection_created\", connection_id=str(conn.id), type=conn_type, env=env)\n    return conn\n\n\ndef list_connections(\n    session: Session,\n    *,\n    conn_type: str | None = None,\n    env: str | None = None,\n) -> list[Connection]:\n    stmt = select(Connection).order_by(Connection.created_at.desc())\n    if conn_type is not None:\n        stmt = stmt.where(Connection.type == conn_type)\n    if env is not None:\n        stmt = stmt.where(Connection.env == env)\n    return list(session.scalars(stmt))\n\n\ndef get_connection(session: Session, connection_id: uuid.UUID) -> Connection:\n    conn = session.get(Connection, connection_id)\n    if conn is None:\n        raise ConnectionNotFoundError(\n            \"connection not found\", detail={\"connection_id\": str(connection_id)}\n        )\n    return conn\n\n\ndef update_connection(\n    session: Session,\n    connection_id: uuid.UUID,\n    *,\n    name: str | None = None,\n    config: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    secret: str | None = None,\n    secret_store: SecretStore,\n) -> Connection:\n    \"\"\"Partial update of name / config / secret. Type and env are immutable.\"\"\"\n    conn = get_connection(session, connection_id)\n\n    if config is not None:\n        _validated_config(conn.type, config)\n        conn.config = config\n    if name is not None:\n        conn.name = name\n    if secret is not None:\n        secret_ref = conn.secret_ref or f\"conn-{conn.id}\"\n        secret_store.set(secret_ref, secret)\n        conn.secret_ref = secret_ref\n\n    session.commit()\n    session.refresh(conn)\n    log.info(\"connection_updated\", connection_id=str(conn.id))\n    return conn\n\n\ndef delete_connection(session: Session, connection_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:\n    conn = get_connection(session, connection_id)\n    session.delete(conn)\n    session.commit()\n    log.info(\"connection_deleted\", connection_id=str(connection_id))\n\n\ndef test_connection(\n    session: Session,\n    connection_id: uuid.UUID,\n    *,\n    secret_store: SecretStore,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Resolve the connection's secret and probe live connectivity.\n\n    Raises `ConnectionTestFailedError` (502) on missing credentials or any\n    adapter-reported connectivity failure.\n    \"\"\"\n    conn = get_connection(session, connection_id)\n    adapter = get_connection_adapter(conn.type)\n\n    if not conn.secret_ref:\n        raise ConnectionTestFailedError(\n            \"connection has no stored credential to test with\",\n            detail={\"connection_id\": str(connection_id)},\n        )\n    try:\n        secret = secret_store.get(conn.secret_ref)\n    except SecretNotFoundError as exc:\n        raise ConnectionTestFailedError(\n            \"credential could not be resolved\", detail={\"connection_id\": str(connection_id)}\n        ) from exc\n\n    try:\n        adapter.test(dict(conn.config), secret)\n    except Exception as exc:\n        log.warning(\n            \"connection_test_failed\", connection_id=str(connection_id), error_type=type(exc).__name__\n        )\n        raise ConnectionTestFailedError(\n            f\"connection test failed: {exc}\", detail={\"connection_id\": str(connection_id)}\n        ) from exc\n\n    log.info(\"connection_test_succeeded\", connection_id=str(connection_id))\n"
}

1	"""Tests for the run/result persistence service.
2	
3	No database or GX: a fake Session records what would be persisted, model
4	instances are built in memory with explicit ids, and a fake CheckRunner returns
5	canned outcomes (or raises). This keeps the service's lifecycle + mapping logic
6	under test independent of Postgres and Snowflake.
7	"""
8	
9	import uuid
10	
11	from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome
12	from backend.app.db.models import Check, Result, Run
13	from backend.app.services import run_service
14	
15	
16	class FakeSession:
17	    """Records add_all'd rows; counts commits/rollbacks. `add_all_raises` simulates
18	    a persistence failure (e.g. DB error) after the adapter has already run."""
19	
20	    def __init__(self, *, add_all_raises: Exception | None = None) -> None:
21	        self.added: list[Result] = []
22	        self.commits = 0
23	        self.rollbacks = 0
24	        self._add_all_raises = add_all_raises
25	
26	    def add_all(self, rows: list[Result]) -> None:
27	        if self._add_all_raises is not None:
28	            raise self._add_all_raises
29	        self.added.extend(rows)
30	
31	    def commit(self) -> None:
32	        self.commits += 1
33	
34	    def rollback(self) -> None:
35	        self.rollbacks += 1
36	
37	
38	class FakeRunner:
39	    def __init__(
40	        self, outcome: SuiteOutcome | None = None, raises: Exception | None = None
41	    ) -> None:
42	        self._outcome = outcome
43	        self._raises = raises
44	        self.called_with: dict[str, object] | None = None
45	
46	    def run_checks(
47	        self, *, table: str, schema: str | None, checks: list[CheckSpec]
48	    ) -> SuiteOutcome:
49	        self.called_with = {"table": table, "schema": schema, "checks": checks}
50	        if self._raises is not None:
51	            raise self._raises
52	        assert self._outcome is not None
53	        return self._outcome
54	
55	
56	def _run() -> Run:
57	    return Run(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=uuid.uuid4(), status="queued")
58	
59	
60	def _checks(n: int) -> list[Check]:
61	    return [
62	        Check(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=uuid.uuid4(), name=f"c{i}", expectation_type="x", config={})
63	        for i in range(n)
64	    ]
65	
66	
67	# ───────────────────────── success path ────────────────────────────
68	
69	
70	def test_successful_run_persists_results_and_marks_succeeded() -> None:
71	    session = FakeSession()
72	    run = _run()
73	    checks = _checks(2)
74	    outcome = SuiteOutcome(
75	        success=False,  # a check failed, but the RUN still executed
76	        checks=[
77	            CheckOutcome("expect_a", success=True, observed_value={"observed_value": 5}),
78	            CheckOutcome(
79	                "expect_b",
80	                success=False,
81	                expected_value={"column": "id"},
82	                sample_failures={"unexpected_count": 1},
83	            ),
84	        ],
85	    )
86	    runner = FakeRunner(outcome=outcome)
87	
88	    result = run_service.execute_run(
89	        session, run=run, checks=checks, runner=runner, table="ORDERS", schema="FIN"
90	    )
91	
92	    assert result is run
93	    assert run.status == "succeeded"  # ran to completion despite a failed check
94	    assert run.started_at is not None and run.finished_at is not None
95	    assert len(session.added) == 2
96	    statuses = {r.check_id: r.status for r in session.added}
97	    assert statuses[checks[0].id] == "passed"
98	    assert statuses[checks[1].id] == "failed"
99	    # adapter received specs derived from the checks + the target table
100	    assert runner.called_with == {
101	        "table": "ORDERS",
102	        "schema": "FIN",
103	        "checks": [CheckSpec("x", {}), CheckSpec("x", {})],
104	    }
105	
106	
107	def test_results_link_to_run_and_check_ids() -> None:
108	    session = FakeSession()
109	    run = _run()
110	    checks = _checks(1)
111	    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome("x", success=True)]))
112	
113	    run_service.execute_run(session, run=run, checks=checks, runner=runner, table="T")
114	
115	    (row,) = session.added
116	    assert row.run_id == run.id
117	    assert row.check_id == checks[0].id
118	
119	
120	# ───────────────────────── NaN sanitisation ────────────────────────
121	
122	
123	def test_nan_in_sample_failures_is_sanitised_before_persist() -> None:
124	    session = FakeSession()
125	    runner = FakeRunner(
126	        SuiteOutcome(
127	            success=False,
128	            checks=[
129	                CheckOutcome(
130	                    "x",
131	                    success=False,
132	                    sample_failures={"partial_unexpected_list": [float("nan"), 2.0]},
133	                )
134	            ],
135	        )
136	    )
137	
138	    run_service.execute_run(session, run=_run(), checks=_checks(1), runner=runner, table="T")
139	
140	    (row,) = session.added
141	    assert row.sample_failures == {"partial_unexpected_list": [None, 2.0]}
142	
143	
144	# ───────────────────────── failure path ────────────────────────────
145	
146	
147	def test_runner_exception_marks_failed_and_persists_no_results() -> None:
148	    session = FakeSession()
149	    run = _run()
150	    runner = FakeRunner(raises=RuntimeError("cannot reach warehouse"))
151	
152	    result = run_service.execute_run(session, run=run, checks=_checks(2), runner=runner, table="T")
153	
154	    assert result.status == "failed"
155	    assert run.finished_at is not None
156	    assert session.added == []  # no half-written results
157	
158	
159	def test_persistence_failure_marks_failed_not_stuck_running() -> None:
160	    """If add_all/commit fails after a successful run, the run must reach a
161	    terminal 'failed' state (not stay 'running') and roll back partial inserts."""
162	    session = FakeSession(add_all_raises=RuntimeError("db connection lost"))
163	    run = _run()
164	    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome("x", success=True)]))
165	
166	    result = run_service.execute_run(session, run=run, checks=_checks(1), runner=runner, table="T")
167	
168	    assert result.status == "failed"
169	    assert run.finished_at is not None
170	    assert session.rollbacks == 1
171	
172	
173	def test_outcome_count_mismatch_marks_failed() -> None:
174	    """zip(strict=True): if the adapter returns the wrong number of outcomes."""
175	    session = FakeSession()
176	    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome("x", success=True)]))
177	
178	    run_service.execute_run(session, run=_run(), checks=_checks(3), runner=runner, table="T")
179	
180	    assert session.added == []
181	
182	
183	def test_empty_run_still_succeeds() -> None:
184	    session = FakeSession()
185	    run = _run()
186	    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[]))
187	
188	    run_service.execute_run(session, run=run, checks=[], runner=runner, table="T")
189	
190	    assert run.status == "succeeded"
191	    assert session.added == []
192

3	import os
4	from collections.abc import Iterator
5	
6	# Set test-mode env vars BEFORE any backend.app.* import resolves. The auth
7	# module computes its mode at import time from settings; without these the
8	# TestClient lifespan would raise 'Auth not configured'.
9	os.environ.setdefault("ENVIRONMENT", "dev")
10	os.environ.setdefault("AUTH_DEV_BYPASS", "true")
11	
12	import pytest

19	def _reset_caches() -> Iterator[None]:

21	    get_settings.cache_clear()

23	    yield
24	    get_settings.cache_clear()
